Then he wrote on the plate all the words of the Buddha that the boy had spoken and an account of his own efforts, and made a determination: “Let this be known only to the king.” Then they departed. When the night had dawned, at the king’s mealtime, the king’s men did not see the plate there. Searching the city, they saw the child and the plate in a cart. They took him along with the plate and showed them to the king. The king saw the letters on the plate and had them read. Pleased with the man’s good qualities, he gave him the position of a great merchant with much honor.

13.

13.

Jinassa nāmaṃ supinena pevaṃ,

Na hoti bhītiṃ lapanena yasmā;

Tasmā munindaṃ satataṃ sarātha,

Guṇe sarantā saraṇañca yāthāti.

Since from uttering the Victor’s name, even in a dream, no fear arises; therefore, always remember the Chief of Sages, recollecting his qualities and going for refuge.

Micchādiṭṭhikassa vatthuṃ dutiyaṃ.

The Second Story of the One with Wrong View.

23. Pādapīṭhikāya vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

23. In the story of the footstool, this is the sequential account.

Jambudīpe [M 0.0084] mahābodhito kira dakkhiṇapasse etaṃ paccantanagaraṃ ahosi. Tattha saddhāsampanno ratanattayamāmako eko upāsako paṭivasati. Tadā eko khīṇāsavo bhagavatā paribhuttaṃ pādapīṭhaṃ thavikāya pakkhipitvā gatagataṭṭhāne pūjento anukkamena taṃ nagaraṃ sampāpuṇitvā sunivattho supāruto pattaṃ gahetvā antaravīthiṃ paṭipajji yugamattadaso pabbajjālīlāya janaṃ paritosento. Atha so upāsako tathā gacchantaṃ theraṃ disvā pasannamānaso upagantvā pañcapatiṭṭhitena vanditvā pattaṃ gahetvā bhojetvā nibaddhaṃ mama gehaṃ āgamanamicchāmi, mamānukampāya ettheva vasatha sāmīti yācitvā nagarāsanne ramaṇīye vanasaṇḍe nadīkūle paṇṇasālaṃ katvā therassa taṃ nīyyātetvā catupaccayehi paṭijagganto mānento pūjento vasati. Theropi tattha phāsukaṭṭhāne bhagavatā paribhuttapādapīṭhadhātuṃ nidhāya vālukāhi thūpaṃ katvā niccaṃ gandhadhūpadīpapupphapūjādīhi pūjayamāno vāsaṃ kappeti. Tasmiṃ samaye tasso pāsakassa anantaragehavāsiko eko issarabhattiko attano devataṃ nibaddhaṃ namassati. Taṃ disvāssa upāsako buddhaguṇe vatvā akhette samma mā viriyaṃ karohi. Pajahetaṃ diṭṭhiṃti āha. Tato so kerāṭiko issarabhattiko ko te satthu guṇānubhāvo, amhākaṃ issarassa guṇova mahantoti vatvā tassa aguṇaṃ guṇanti kathento āha.

It is said that in Jambudīpa, to the south of the great Bodhi tree, there was a border town. A lay follower lived there, endowed with faith and devoted to the Triple Gem. At that time, an arahant, who had placed a footstool used by the Blessed One in a bag, was traveling from place to place venerating it. In due course he arrived at that town. Properly dressed and robed, he took his bowl and entered the main street, looking a yoke’s length ahead and delighting the people with the grace of the renunciant life. Seeing the elder walking in this way, the lay follower, with a confident mind, approached him, paid homage with the five-point prostration, took his bowl, and offered him food. Then he made a request: “Venerable sir, I wish you to come to my house regularly. Out of compassion for me, please reside right here.” He then built a leaf-hut for the elder in a delightful forest grove on a riverbank near the town, presented it to him, and looked after him with the four requisites, honoring and venerating him. The elder, in that comfortable place, enshrined the relic—the footstool used by the Blessed One—built a stūpa of sand over it, and took up his residence there, constantly honoring it with offerings of incense, lamps, flowers, and the like. At that time, a next-door neighbor of that lay follower, a devotee of Isvara, regularly paid homage to his own deity. Seeing this, the lay follower spoke of the Buddha’s qualities and said: “Friend, do not exert your energy in an unproductive field. Abandon this wrong view.” Then that obstinate devotee of Isvara replied: “What is the virtuous power of your teacher? The qualities of our lord Isvara are greater!” And speaking of its demerits as if they were merits, he said:

1.

1.

Tipuraṃ so vināsesi, lalāṭanayanagginā;

Asureca vināsesi, tisūlena mahissaro.

“He destroyed Tripura with the fire from the eye on his forehead; the great lord Isvara destroyed the asuras with his trident.

2.

2.

Jaṭākalāpamāvattaṃ, naccatī dinasandhiyaṃ;

Vādeti bherivīṇādiṃ, gītaṃcāpi sa gāyati.

With his coil of matted hair swirling, he dances at twilight; he plays the drum, the lute, and other instruments, and he also sings songs.

3.

3.

Bhariyāyo [M 0.0085] tassa tisso, jaṭāyekaṃ samubbahe;

Ekamekena passena, passamāno carekakaṃ.

He has three wives; one he carries in his matted hair. He wanders alone, looking to one side.

4.

4.

Hatthicammambaradharo, teneva vāritā tapo;

Asādisehi puttehi, rūpena ca supākaṭo.

He wears a garment of elephant hide, by which his ascetic power is shielded. He has incomparable sons and is renowned for his handsome form.

5.

5.

Ratiyā ca madhupāne ca, byāvaṭo sabbadā ca so;

Manussaṭṭhidharo sīsa, kapālenesa bhuñjaki.

He is always engaged in sensual pleasure and drinking intoxicants; carrying human bones, he eats from a skull.

6.

6.

Na jāto na bhayaṃ tassa, maraṇaṃ natthi sassato;

Īdiso me mahādevo, natthaññassīdiso guṇoti.

He is unborn, he has no fear, for him there is no death; he is eternal. Such is my great god; no other has such qualities as these.”

Taṃ sutvā upāsako samma tuyhaṃ issarassa ete guṇā nāma tāva hontu. Aguṇā nāma kittakā hontīti vatvā bhagavato sakalaguṇe saṃharitvā kathento āha.

Hearing that, the lay follower said: “Friend, let these be the so-called qualities of your lord Isvara. But how many demerits does he have?” Then, summarizing all the qualities of the Blessed One, he said:

7.

7.

Loke sabbasavantīnaṃ, ādhāro sāgaro yathā;

Sabbesaṃ guṇarāsīnaṃ, ādhārova tathāgato.

“Just as the ocean is the receptacle for all the rivers in the world, so the Tathāgata is the receptacle for all accumulations of good qualities.

8.

8.

Carācarānaṃ sabbesaṃ, ādhārāva dharā ayaṃ;

Tathā guṇānaṃ sabbesaṃ, ādhārova tathāgato.

Just as this earth is the support for all things moving and unmoving, so the Tathāgata is the support for all good qualities.

9.

9.

Evaṃ santo viyattoca viyanto itikatthaci, evaṃ so karuṇāparo;

Eva middhividhā tassa, evamevaṃ guṇā iti.

Thus he is peaceful and wise, thus he is full of compassion; such are his kinds of psychic power, such indeed are his qualities.

10.

10.

Buddhopi sakkoti na yassa vaṇṇe,

Kappampi vatvā khayataṃ gametuṃ;

Pageva ca brahmasurā surehi,

Vattuṃ na hānantaguṇassa vaṇṇanti.

Even a Buddha, speaking for an entire eon, could not come to the end of describing his praise; how much less then could Brahmā, devas, and asuras describe the praise of him whose qualities are endless.”

Evaṃ vadantā pana te ubhopi amhākaṃ devo uttamo amhākaṃ devo uttamoti kalahaṃ vaḍḍhetvā rañño santikamagamaṃsu, rājā [M 0.0086] tesaṃ kathaṃ sutvā tenahi tumhākaṃ devatānaṃ mahantabhāvaṃ iddhipāṭihāriyena jānissāma. Dassetha tehi no iddhiṃti nagare bheriṃ carāpesi. Ito kira sattāhaccayena imesaṃ dvinnaṃ satthārānaṃ pāṭihāriyāni bhavissanti. Sabbe sannipatantūti taṃ sutvā nānādisāsu bahū manussā samāgamiṃsu, atha micchādiṭṭhikā ajja amhākaṃ devassa ānubhāvaṃ passāmāti mahantaṃ pūjaṃ karonto tattha sāraṃ nāddasaṃsu, sammādiṭṭhikāpi ajja amhākaṃ bhagavato ānubhāvaṃ passissāmāti vāḷukāthūpaṃ gantvā gandhamālādīhi pūjetvā padakkhiṇaṃ katvā añjalimpaggayha aṭṭhaṃsu. Atha rājāpi balavāhanaparivuto ekamante aṭṭhāsi. Nānāsamayavādinopi ajja tesaṃ pāṭihāriyaṃ passissāmāti mañcātimañcaṃ katvā aṭṭhaṃsu. Tesaṃ samāgame sammādiṭṭhikā vāḷukāthūpa mabhimukhaṃ katvā añjalimpaggayha sāmi amhākaṃ bhagavā sabbabuddhakiccāni niṭṭhāpetvā anupādisesāya nibbāṇadhātuyā parinibbāyi. Sāriputtamahāmoggallānādayo asītimahāsāvakāpi parinibbāyiṃsu, natthettha amhākaṃ aññaṃ paṭisaraṇanti vatvā saccakiriyaṃ karontā āhaṃsu.

As they were speaking thus, the two of them escalated their quarrel, each saying, “Our deity is supreme! Our deity is supreme!” and went to the king. Having heard their case, the king said: “Very well, we shall know the greatness of your deities through a display of psychic power. Show us your power!” He had a drum beaten through the city, proclaiming: “Seven days from now, there will be a display of miracles by these two teachers. Let everyone assemble!” Hearing this, many people gathered from various directions. Then those with wrong view, thinking, “Today we shall see the power of our god,” made great offerings but saw no substance in them. Those with right view also thought, “Today we shall see the power of our Blessed One,” and went to the sand stūpa. They honored it with perfumes, garlands, and so on, circumambulated it, and stood with their hands raised in reverence. The king, too, surrounded by his army and vehicles, stood to one side. Adherents of various other doctrines also, thinking, “Today we shall see their miracle,” set up platforms and pavilions and stood waiting. At their gathering, those with right view faced the sand stūpa, raised their hands in reverence, and said: “Venerable Sirs, our Blessed One, having completed all the duties of a Buddha, has attained final Nibbāna with the Nibbāna-element without residue remaining. The eighty great disciples, headed by Sāriputta and Mahāmoggallāna, have also attained final Nibbāna. We have no other refuge here.” Then, making an asseveration of truth, they said:

11.

11.

Āpāṇakoṭiṃ buddhassa, saraṇaṃ no gatā yadi;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

“If, until the end of our lives, we have gone for refuge to the Buddha, by that truth may this relic display a miracle.

12.

12.

Āpāṇakoṭiṃ dhammassa, saraṇaṃ no gatā yadi;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

If, until the end of our lives, we have gone for refuge to the Dhamma, by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

13.

13.

Āpāṇakoṭiṃ saṅghassa, saraṇaṃ no gatā yadi;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

If, until the end of our lives, we have gone for refuge to the Sangha, by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

14.

14.

Rāmakāle munindassa, pādukā cāsi abbhutā;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

In the time of Rāma, the chief of sages, there was a marvelous sandal; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

15.

15.

Chaddantakāle [M 0.0087] munino, dāṭhā charaṃsirañjitā;

Tena saccenayaṃ dhātu, nicchāretu cha raṃsiyo.

In the time of Chaddanta, the tusk of the sage was adorned with six-colored rays; by this truth, may this relic emit six rays.

16.

16.

Jātamatto tadā buddho, ṭhito paṅkajamuddhani;

Nicchāresāsabhiṃvācaṃ, aggo seṭṭhotiādinā;

Tena saccenayaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

As soon as he was born, the Buddha stood on top of a lotus and uttered the bull-like speech beginning, ‘I am the foremost, I am the best’; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

17.

17.

Nimitte caturo disvā, nikkhanto abhinikkhamaṃ;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

Having seen the four signs, he went forth in the Great Renunciation; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

18.

18.

Mārasenaṃ palāpetvā, nisinno bujjhi bodhiyaṃ;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

Having put Māra’s army to flight, he sat and attained awakening at the Bodhi tree; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

19.

19.

Dhammacakkaṃ pavattesi, jino sipatane tadā;

Tena saccena yaṃ dhātu, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃ.

The Victor then set in motion the Wheel of Dhamma at Isipatana; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.

20.

20.

Nandopanandabhogindaṃ, nāgaṃ nālāgirivhayaṃ;

Āḷavakā dayo yakkhe, brahmāno ca bakā dayo.

Nandopananda the serpent king, the nāga named Nālāgiri, the yakkhas headed by Āḷavaka, and the brahmās headed by Baka,

21.

21.

Saccakādinigaṇṭheca, kūṭadantā dayo dvije;

Damesi tena saccena, dassetu pāṭihāriyaṃti.

the Nigaṇṭhas headed by Saccaka, and the brahmins headed by Kūṭadanta—all these he tamed; by that truth, may this relic display a miracle.”

Evañca pana vatvā upāsakā amhākaṃ anukammaṃ paṭicca mahājanassa micchādiṭṭhibhedanattaṃ pāṭihāriyaṃ dassetha sāmīti ārādhesuṃ. Atha buddhānubhāvañca therānubhāvañca upāsakānaṃ saccakiriyānubhāvañca paṭicca vāḷukāthūpaṃ dvidhā bhinditvā pādapīṭhadhātu ākāsa mabbhuggantvā chabbaṇṇaraṃsiyo vissajjentī vilāsamānā aṭṭhāsi. Atha mahājanā celukkhepasahassāni pavattentā sādhukīḷhaṃ kīḷantā mahānādaṃ pavattentā mahantaṃ pūjamakaṃsu. Micchādiṭṭhikāpi imaṃ acchariyaṃ disvā vimhitamānasā micchādiṭṭhiṃ bhinditvā ratanattayaparāyaṇā saraṇa magamaṃsūti.

Having spoken thus, the lay followers implored: “Out of compassion for us, for the sake of shattering the wrong views of the great multitude, please display a miracle, Lord!” Then, on account of the power of the Buddha, the power of the elder, and the power of the lay followers’ asseveration of truth, the sand stūpa split in two. The footstool-relic rose up into the sky and stood there shining, emitting six-colored rays. Then the great crowd, waving thousands of cloths, raising a great shout, and acclaiming it joyfully, made a great offering. Seeing this marvel, those with wrong view were astonished. They shattered their wrong view and, with the Triple Gem as their destination, went for refuge.

22.

22.

Phuṭṭhopi [M 0.0088] pādena jinassa evaṃ,

Kaliṅgaro pā si mahānubhāvo;

Lokekanāthassa anāsavassa,

Mahānubhāvo hi acintanīyoti.

Thus, being touched by the Victor’s foot, the footstool became one of great power. Truly, the great power of the world’s sole protector, the one without taints, is inconceivable.

Pādapīṭhikāya vatthuṃ tatiyaṃ.

The Third Story of the Footstool.

24. Uttarasāmaṇerassa vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

24. This is the sequential account in the story of the novice Uttara.

So kira purimabuddhesu purimabuddhe itisabbattha katādhikāro tattha tattha bhave vivaṭṭūpanissayaṃ puññaṃ upacinanto sumedhassa bhagavato kāle vijjādharo hutvā himavati paṭivasati. Tadā sumedho nāma sammāsambuddho viveka manubrūhanto himavantaṃ gantvā ramaṇīye padese pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvā nisīdi. Tadā vijjādharo ākāsena gacchanto chabbaṇṇaraṃsīhi virājamānaṃ bhagavantaṃ disvā tīhi kaṇikārapupphehi pūjesi, pupphāni buddhānubhāvena satthu upari chattākārena aṭṭhaṃsu, so tena bhīyyoso mattāya pasannacitto hutvā aparabhāge kālaṃkatvā tāvatiṃsabhavane nibbattitvā uḷāraṃ dibbasampattimanubhavanto yāvatāyukaṃ tattha ṭhatvā tato cuto devamanussesu saṃsaranto imasmiṃ buddhuppāde rājagahanagare brāhmaṇamahāsālassa putto hutvā nibbatti. Uttarotissa nāmaṃ ahosi. So uttamarūpadharo viññutaṃ patto brāhmaṇavijjāsu nipphattiṃ patvā jātiyā rūpena vijjāya sīlācārena ca lokassa mahanīyo jāto, tassa taṃ paññāsampattiṃdisvā vassakāro magadhamahāmatto attano dhītaraṃ dātukāmo hutvā attano adhippāyaṃ pavedesi. So nissaraṇajjhāsayatāya taṃ paṭikkhipitvā kālenakālaṃ dhammasenāpatiṃ payirupāsanto tassa [M 0.0089] santike dhammaṃ sutvā paṭiladdhasaddho pabbajitvā vattasampanno hutvā theraṃ upaṭṭhahati. Tena ca samayena therassa aññataro ābādho uppanno hoti, tassa bhesajjatthāya uttarasāmaṇero pātova pattacīvara mādāya vihārato nikkhamma antarāmagge taḷākassa tīre pattaṃ ṭhapetvā udakasamīpaṃ gantvā mukhaṃ dhovati, tadā aññataro ummaggacoro katakammo ārakkhapurisehi anubaddho aggadvāreneva nagarato nikkhamitvā palāyanto attanā gahitaṃ ratanabhaṇḍikaṃ sāmaṇerassa patte pakkhipitvā palāyi. So sāmaṇeropi pattasamīpaṃ sattusamīpaṃ itipi katthaci upagato hoti, coraṃ anubandhantā rājapurisā sāmaṇerassa patte bhaṇḍikaṃ disvā ayaṃ coro, iminā coriyaṃ katanti sāmaṇeraṃ pacchābāhaṃ bandhitvā vassakārassa brāhmaṇassa dassesuṃ. Vassakāro ca tadā rañño vinicchaye niyutto hutvā chejjabhejjaṃ anusāsati. So eso pubbe mama vacanaṃ nādiyi. Suddhapāsaṇḍiyesu pabbajīti ca baddhāghātattā taṃ kammaṃ asodhetvāva jīvantamevetaṃ sūle uttāsethāti āṇā pesi, rājapurisā taṃ nimbasūle uttāsesuṃ. Sāmaṇero sūlagge nisinno upajjhāyassa me ko bhesajjaṃ āharissatīti sāriputtattheraṃ sari. Tato thero taṃ pavattiṃ ñatvā sammāsambuddhassa kathesi, bhagavāpi mahāsāvakaparivuto tassa ñāṇaparipākaṃ oloketvā taṃ ṭhānamagamāsi. Tato bhagavato nikkhantabhāvā sakalanagare kolāhalaṃ ahosi, mahājanakāyo sannipati. Atha bhagavā vipphurantahatthatale nakhamaṇimayūkhasambhinnapītābhāsatāya paggharantajātihiṅgulakasuvaṇṇarasadhārāviya jālāvaguṇṭhitamudutalunaṅgulaṃ hatthaṃ uttarassa sīse ṭhapetvā uttara idaṃ te pubbe katapāpakammassa phalaṃ uppannaṃ, tattha tayā paccavekkhaṇabalena adhivāsanā kātabbāti āha. Teneva āha.

It is said that he, having made an aspiration under former Buddhas, accumulated merit in various existences as a basis for liberation. In the time of the Blessed One Sumedha, he was a knowledge-holder living in the Himalayas. At that time, the Perfectly Enlightened One named Sumedha, cultivating seclusion, went to the Himalayas and sat down cross-legged in a pleasant spot. Then the knowledge-holder, traveling through the sky, saw the Blessed One shining with six-colored rays and honored him with three kaṇikāra flowers. By the Buddha’s power, the flowers remained above the Teacher like a canopy. With his mind filled with even greater confidence, he passed away in due course and was reborn in the Tāvatiṃsa heaven, where he enjoyed abundant divine prosperity for his entire lifespan. After passing away from there, he wandered among gods and humans until, during this Buddha-dispensation, he was reborn in Rājagaha as the son of a wealthy brahmin. His name was Uttara. Endowed with supreme beauty and having attained understanding, he achieved mastery in the brahminical sciences and became highly esteemed in the world for his birth, appearance, knowledge, and virtuous conduct. Seeing his accomplishment in wisdom, Vassakāra, the chief minister of Magadha, wished to give him his daughter in marriage and expressed his intention. But he, being intent on renunciation, refused her. From time to time, he attended upon the General of the Dhamma, and after hearing the Dhamma from him, he gained faith, went forth, became accomplished in the observances, and served the elder. At that time, a certain illness arose in the elder. To fetch medicine for him, the novice Uttara took his bowl and robe early in the morning and left the monastery. On the way, he set his bowl down by a pond and went to the water’s edge to wash his face. Just then, a notorious thief who had committed a crime, pursued by the king's men, fled the city by the main gate. As he ran, he dropped a bundle of stolen jewels into the novice’s bowl and fled. When the novice returned to his bowl, the king's men chasing the thief saw the bundle in his bowl and said, 'This is the thief! He committed the theft!' They bound the novice’s hands behind his back and presented him to the brahmin Vassakāra. Vassakāra was at that time appointed by the king to pass judgment and would prescribe punishments of mutilation. Harboring a grudge because 'he previously did not accept my word and went forth among mere sectarians,' Vassakāra, without investigating the matter, sent down the order: 'Impale this one alive on a stake!' The king's men impaled him on a stake of neem-wood. Seated atop the stake, the novice thought, 'Who will bring medicine for my preceptor?' and he recollected the Elder Sāriputta. The elder, learning of the situation, reported it to the Perfectly Enlightened One. The Blessed One, surrounded by his great disciples, saw the maturity of his knowledge and went to that place. When the Blessed One departed, a great uproar arose in the entire city, and a great crowd of people gathered. Then the Blessed One placed his hand—soft and tender, its fingers veiled in a net of light, shimmering with a yellow luster mingled with rays from his jeweled nails, like streams of flowing vermillion and liquid gold—upon Uttara’s head and said: 'Uttara, this is the result of an evil kamma you did in the past that has arisen; you must endure it with the power of reflection.' And so he said:

1.

1.

Atīte [M 0.0090] kira ekasmiṃ, gāme tva masi dārako;

Dārekehi samāgamma, kīḷanto keḷimaṇḍale.

In the past, it is said, you were a boy in a certain village; gathering with other boys, you played in a playground.

2.

2.

Gahetvā sukhumaṃ sūkaṃ, tadā tvaṃ nimbajalliyā;

Uttāsesi tattha sūle, jīvamānakamakkhikaṃ.

Taking a fine spike from a neem twig, you then impaled a living fly on a stake.

3.

3.

Aparampi te pāpakammaṃ, pavakkhāmi suṇohi me;

Ovadantiṃ hitena tvaṃ, atīte sakamātaraṃ.

I will tell you of another evil kamma of yours; listen to me. In the past, your own mother advised you for your welfare,

4.

4.

Jīvasūle nisīdāti, kopenābhisapī tuvaṃ;

Imehi dvīhi pāpehi, saraṃ saṃsārasāgare.

but you cursed her in anger, saying: 'May you sit on a living stake!' Because of these two evil deeds, you have wandered in the ocean of saṃsāra.

5.

5.

Pañcajātisate acchi, jīvasūlamhi nimbaje;

Ayaṃ te carimā jāti, etthāpica vipacci soti.

For five hundred births you have been on a living stake made of neem. This is your final birth, and here too that kamma has ripened.”

Evamādinā nayena tassa ajjhāsayānurūpena dhammaṃ desesi, uttaro amatābhisekasadisena satthuno hatthasamphassasañjātapasādasomanassatāya uḷāraṃ pītipāmojjaṃ paṭilabhitvā yathāparicitaṃ vipassanāmaggaṃ samārūḷho ñāṇassa paripākaṃ gatattā satthu desanāvilāsena maggapaṭipāṭiyā sabbakilese khepetvā chaḷabhiñño ahosi. Dhammaṃ sutvā tattha samāgatānaṃ devamanussānaṃ caturāsītipāṇasahassānaṃ dhammābhisamayo ahosīti vadanti. Uttaro pana chaḷabhiñño hutvā sūlato uṭṭhahitvā ākāse ṭhatvā pāṭihāriyaṃ dassesi. Mahājanā acchariyabbhutacittā jātā ahesuṃ. Tāvadevassa vaṇorundhi, so bhikkhūhi āvuso tādisaṃ dukkhaṃ anubhavanto kathaṃ tvaṃ vipassanaṃ anuyuñjituṃ sakkhīti puṭṭho pageva me āvuso saṃsāre ādīnavo saṅkhārānañca sabhāvo sudiṭṭho. Tasmāhaṃ tādisaṃ dukkhaṃ anubhavantopi asakkhiṃ vipassanaṃ vaḍḍhetvā visesaṃ adhigantuṃti āha. Athāparabhāge so bhikkhusaṅghamajjhe attano pubbacaritā padānaṃ pakāsento imā gāthā abhāsi.

In this way, the Teacher taught the Dhamma in accordance with his disposition. Through the touch of the Teacher’s hand, which was like an ambrosial consecration, Uttara experienced profound joy and delight. Having entered upon the path of insight as he had practiced it, and because his knowledge had reached maturity, through the grace of the Teacher's teaching, following the sequence of the path, he eradicated all defilements and became endowed with the six higher knowledges. It is said that upon hearing the Dhamma, eighty-four thousand beings—gods and humans—who had gathered there attained the vision of the Dhamma. Then Uttara, having become endowed with the six higher knowledges, rose from the stake, stood in the air, and displayed a miracle. The great crowd was struck with amazement and wonder. At that very moment, his wound healed. When the bhikkhus asked him, 'Friend, while enduring such suffering, how were you able to devote yourself to insight?' he replied, 'Friends, the danger in saṃsāra and the true nature of formations were already clearly seen by me. Therefore, even while enduring such suffering, I was able to develop insight and attain a special distinction.' Later, in the midst of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus, while disclosing the account of his own past actions, he recited these verses.

6.

6.

Sumedho [M 0.0091] nāma sambuddho, dvattiṃsavaralakkhaṇo;

Vivekakāmo sambuddho, himavanta mupāgami.

The Perfectly Enlightened One named Sumedha, possessing the thirty-two excellent marks, desiring seclusion, approached the Himālaya.

7.

7.

Ajjhogahetvā himavantaṃ, aggo kāruṇiko muni;

Pallaṅkaṃ ābhujitvāna, nisīdi purisuttamo.

Having entered the Himālaya, the foremost compassionate sage, the supreme person sat down, having folded his legs crosswise.

8.

8.

Vijjādharo tadā āsiṃ, antalikkhacaro ahaṃ;

Tisūlaṃ sukataṃ gayha, gacchāmi ambare tadā.

I was a knowledge-holder then, a sky-farer was I; taking up a well-made trident, I would travel through the sky then.

9.

9.

Pabbatagge yathā aggi, puṇṇamāseva candimā;

Vanaṃ obhāsate buddho, sālarājāva phullito.

On a mountain peak like a fire, or the moon on its full-moon day, the Buddha illuminates the forest like a blossoming kingly sāl tree.

10.

10.

Vanaggā nikkhamitvāna, buddharaṃsī vidhāvare;

Nalaggivaṇṇasaṅkāsā, disvā cittaṃ pasādayiṃ.

Issuing from the forest top, the Buddha’s rays spread about, resembling the color of a reed-fire; seeing them, my mind grew confident.

11.

11.

Vicinaṃ addasaṃ pupphaṃ, kaṇikāraṃ devagandhikaṃ;

Tīṇi pupphāni ādāya, buddhaseṭṭhaṃ apūjayiṃ.

Searching about, I saw a flower, a kaṇikāra with a divine fragrance; taking three blossoms, I honored the best of Buddhas.

12.

12.

Buddhassa ānubhāvena, tīṇi pupphāni me tadā;

Uddhavaṇṭā adhopattā, chāyaṃ kubbanti satthuno.

By the Buddha’s power, those three flowers of mine, with stalks upright and petals down, formed a canopy for the Teacher.

13.

13.

Tena kammena sukatena, cetanāpaṇidhīhica;

Jahitvā mānusaṃ dehaṃ, tāvatiṃsamagañchahaṃ.

By that well-done kamma, and by my volitions and aspirations, having abandoned the human body, I went to the Tāvatiṃsa heaven.

14.

14.

Tattha me sukataṃ byamhaṃ, kaṇikārīti ñāyati;

Saṭṭhiyojana mubbedhaṃ, tiṃsayojanavitthataṃ.

There a well-made celestial mansion was mine, known as 'Kaṇikārī'; it was sixty yojanas in height and thirty yojanas in breadth.

15.

15.

Sahassakhaṇḍaṃ satabheṇḍu, dhajālu haritāmayaṃ;

Satasahassāni byūhāni, byamhe pāturahaṃsu me.

With a thousand stories and a hundred pinnacles, bannered and made of emerald, a hundred thousand pavilions appeared for me in that mansion.

16.

16.

Soṇṇamayā maṇimayā, lohitaṅkamayā pica;

Phalikā pica pallaṅkā, yadicchaka yadicchakā.

There were couches of gold, of gems, and of rubies too, and crystal couches, appearing just as I wished.

17.

17.

Mahārahañca sayanaṃ, tūlikaṃ vikatīyakaṃ;

Uddalomikaekantaṃ, bimbohanasamāyutaṃ.

And a costly bed with a cotton mattress and fine coverlets, with a long-fleeced rug on one side, and furnished with pillows.

18.

18.

Bhavanā [M 0.0092] nikkhamitvāna, caranto devacārikaṃ;

Yadā icchāmi gamanaṃ, devasaṃghapurakkhato.

Leaving my dwelling, wandering on a celestial journey, whenever I wished to go, I was escorted by a host of devas.

19.

19.

Pupphassa heṭṭhā tiṭṭhāmi, uparicchadanaṃ mama;

Samantā yojanasataṃ, kaṇikārehi chāditaṃ.

I stand beneath the flower, my canopy above; for a hundred yojanas all around, it is covered with kaṇikāra blossoms.

20.

20.

Saṭṭhituriyasahassāni, sāyaṃ pātaṃ upaṭṭhahuṃ;

Parivārenti maṃ niccaṃ, rattindivamatanditā.

Sixty thousand musical instruments attended me evening and morning; they constantly surrounded me, untiring day and night.

21.

21.

Tattha naccehi gītehi, tālehi vāditehi ca;

Ramāmi khiḍḍāratiyā, modāmi kāmakāmahaṃ.

There, with dances, songs, clapping, and instrumental music, I delight in the pleasure of sport; I rejoice, indulging my sensual desires.

22.

22.

Tattha bhutvā ca pitvā ca, modāmi tidase tadā;

Nārīgaṇehi sahito, modāmi byamhamuttame.

There, having eaten and drunk, I rejoice then among the gods; accompanied by hosts of women, I rejoice in that supreme mansion.

23.

23.

Satānaṃ pañcakkhattuñca, devarajja makārayiṃ;

Satānaṃ tīṇikkhattuṃca, cakkavattī ahosahaṃ;

Padesarajjaṃ vipulaṃ, gaṇanāto asaṃkhiyaṃ.

Five hundred times I reigned as king of the devas; three hundred times I was a wheel-turning monarch; and my reigns over vast regional kingdoms are beyond calculation.

24.

24.

Bhavābhave saṃsaranto, mahābhogaṃ labhāmahaṃ;

Bhoge me ūnatā natthi, buddhapūjāyi daṃ phalaṃ.

Wandering from existence to existence, I obtain great wealth; I have no lack of possessions—this is the fruit of that worship of the Buddha.

25.

25.

Dve me bhave saṃsarāmi, devatte atha mānuse;

Aññaṃ gatiṃ na jānāmi, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

I wander in two states of existence only, the divine and the human; I know no other destination—this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

26.

26.

Dve me kule pajānāmi, khattiye cāpi brāhmaṇe;

Nīce kule na jānāmi, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

I have been born into only two families, the noble and the brahmin; I have not been born into a low family—this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

27.

27.

Hatthiyānaṃ assayānaṃ, sivikaṃ sandamānikaṃ;

Labhāmi sabbameve taṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

Elephant-vehicles, horse-vehicles, palanquins, and litters—I obtain all these: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

28.

28.

Dāsīgaṇaṃ dāsagaṇaṃ, nāriyo ca alaṅkatā;

Labhāmi sabba meve taṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

Groups of maidservants and menservants, and women beautifully adorned; I obtain all this: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

29.

29.

Koseyyakambaliyāni, khomakappāsikānica;

Labhāmi sabbamevetaṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

Garments of silk and wool, of linen and cotton; I obtain all this: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

30.

30.

Navavatthaṃ [M 0.0093] navaphalaṃ, navaggarasabhojanaṃ;

Labhāmi sabbamevetaṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

New garments, fresh fruits, and food with the choicest new flavors; I obtain all this: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

31.

31.

Imaṃ khāda imaṃ bhuñja, imamhi sayane saya;

Labhāmi sabbamevetaṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

“Eat this, enjoy this, lie on this couch”—I obtain all this: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

32.

32.

Sabbattha pūjito homi, yaso accuggato mama;

Mahesakkho ghadā homi, abhejjapariso sadā;

Ñātīnaṃ uttamo homi, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

I am honored everywhere, my fame has risen high; I am always powerful, with a retinue ever indivisible; I am the foremost among my kinsmen: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

33.

33.

Sītaṃ uṇhaṃ na jānāmi, pariḷāho na vijjati;

Atho cetasikaṃ dukkhaṃ, hadaye me na vijjati.

I do not experience cold or heat, no burning torment is found; nor is any mental suffering found within my heart.

34.

34.

Suvaṇṇavaṇṇo hutvāna, saṃsarāmi bhavābhave;

Vevaṇṇiyaṃ na jānāmi, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

Having a golden complexion, I wander through successive existences; I know no disfigurement: this is the fruit of worshipping the Buddha.

35.

35.

Devalokā cavitvāna, sukkamūlena codito;

Sāvatthiyaṃ pure jāto, mahāsāle suaḍḍhake.

Having passed away from the world of the devas, impelled by my wholesome roots, I was born in the city of Sāvatthī in a very wealthy great sāl family.

36.

36.

Pañcakāmaguṇe hitvā, pabbajiṃanagāriyaṃ;

Jātiyā sattavassohaṃ, arahattamapāpuṇiṃ.

Having abandoned the five strands of sensual pleasure, I went forth into the homeless life; at seven years of age, I attained arahantship.

37.

37.

Upasampādayī buddho, guṇamaññāya cakkhumā;

Taruṇova pūjanīyo haṃ, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

The Buddha, the One with Vision, gave me the higher ordination, having recognized my good qualities. Though young, I am worthy of veneration: this is the fruit of honoring the Buddha.

38.

38.

Dibbacakkhuṃ visuddhaṃ me, samādhikusalo ahaṃ;

Abhiññāpāramippatto, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

My divine eye is purified, I am skilled in concentration; I have reached the perfection of the direct knowledges: this is the fruit of honoring the Buddha.

39.

39.

Paṭisambhidā anuppatto, iddhipādesu kovido;

Saddhamme pāramippatto, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

I have attained the analytical knowledges and am skilled in the bases of spiritual power; I have reached perfection in the good Dhamma: this is the fruit of honoring the Buddha.

40.

40.

Tiṃsakappasahassamhi, yaṃ buddhamabhipūjayiṃ;

Duggatiṃ nābhijānāmi, buddhapūjāyidaṃ phalaṃ.

For thirty thousand aeons, since I honored the Buddha, I have known no bad destination: this is the fruit of honoring the Buddha.

41.

41.

Kilesā jhāpitā mayhaṃ, bhavā sabbe samūhatā;

Nāgova bandhanaṃ chetvā, viharāmi anāsavo.

My defilements are burnt up, all kinds of becoming are eradicated. Like a great elephant that has broken its fetters, I dwell free from taints.

42.

42.

Svāgataṃ [M 0.0094] vata me āsi, buddhaseṭṭhassa santikaṃ;

Tisso vijjā anuppatto, kataṃ buddhassa sāsanaṃ.

Fortunate indeed was my coming into the presence of the best of Buddhas. I have attained the three true knowledges; the Buddha’s teaching has been fulfilled.

43.

43.

Paṭisambhidā catasso ca, vimekkhā pica aṭṭhime;

Chaḷabhiññā sacchikatā, kataṃ buddhassa sāsanaṃti;

Taṃ sutvā bahū kusalakammaparāyaṇā ahesuṃ.

“The four analytical knowledges, the eight liberations, and the six direct knowledges have been realized by me; the Buddha’s teaching has been fulfilled.” Hearing this, many became intent on wholesome actions.

44.

44.

Sahetukā pacchimikāpi sattā,

Pāpaṃ na sakkonti jahātumevaṃ;

Anicchamānehi janehi dukkhaṃ,

Ārāva pāpaṃ parivajjanīyaṃti.

Even later beings who have the conditions are not able to abandon evil in this way. Since people do not desire suffering, evil should be shunned from afar.

Uttarasāmaṇerassa vatthuṃ catutthaṃ.

The fourth story: that of the novice Uttara.

25. Kavīrapaṭṭana vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

25. This is the sequential narrative in the story of Kāvīrapaṭṭana.

Jambudīpe kira coḷaraṭṭhe kāvīrapaṭṭanaṃ nāma ahosi. Tattha māhissarikā bahū micchādiṭṭhikā vasanti. Tatthekasmiṃ devālaye cittakammaṃ karontā ekasmiṃ phalake issarassa onamitvā vandanākāraṃ bhagavato rūpaṃ akaṃsu. Tasmiṃ samaye tattha bahū upāsakā taṃ devakulaṃ gantvā tattha tattha cittakammāni olokentā tasmiṃ phalake taṃ cittakammaṃ addasaṃsu. Disvāna te aho amhehi apassitabbaṃ passitaṃ. Sadevake loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyāsadevamanussāya pajāya ca aparimāṇesu cakkavāḷesu bhagavato uttaritaraṃ ṭhapetvā samasamopi natthi. Sakalehi sattanikāyehi vandanīyo pūjanīyo bhagavā. Ananurūpaṃ tassa etehi kataṃti rodantā paridevantā rājadvāraṃ gantvā ugghosesuṃ, taṃ sutvā rājā te pakkosāpetvā kasmā tumhe [M 0.0095] ugghosethāti pucchi, te eva māhaṃsu. Deva amhākaṃ bhagavā devātidevo sakkātisakko brahmātibrahmā meruva acalo sāgaro gambhīro ākāsova ananto pathavīva patthaṭotiādīhi bhagavato guṇaṃ vaṇṇesuṃ. Tena vuttaṃ apadāne.

It is said that in Jambudīpa, in the Coḷa country, there was a city named Kāvīrapaṭṭana. Many powerful people holding wrong views lived there. In one of their temples, while painting murals, they depicted on a panel an image of the Blessed One in a posture of bowing down and venerating the god Issara. At that time, many lay followers went to that temple and, while looking at the various paintings, they saw that particular mural. Having seen it, they cried out: 'Alas, we have seen what should not be seen! In this world with its devas, Māra, and Brahmā, in this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans, and in countless world-systems, there is no one equal to the Blessed One, let alone superior. The Blessed One is worthy of veneration and honor by all classes of beings. What these people have done is unfitting!' Weeping and lamenting, they went to the palace gate and raised an outcry. Hearing this, the king had them summoned and asked, 'Why do you raise an outcry?' They said: 'Your Majesty, our Blessed One is the deva beyond devas, the Sakka beyond Sakkas, the Brahmā beyond Brahmās. He is unshakeable like Mount Meru, profound like the ocean, infinite like space, and extensive like the earth.' In this way they extolled the qualities of the Blessed One. Thus it is said in the Apadāna:

1.

1.

Battiṃsalakkhaṇadharo, sunakkhattova candimā;

Anubyañjanasampanno, sālarājāva phullito.

Bearer of the thirty-two marks, like the moon in a fair constellation; endowed with the secondary characteristics, like a sāl-tree king in full bloom.

2.

2.

Raṃsijālaparikkhitto, dittova kanakācalo;

Byāmappabhāparivuto, sataraṃsi divākaro.

Encircled by a net of rays, blazing like a mountain of gold; surrounded by a fathom-wide aura, like the sun, the maker of day.

3.

3.

Soṇṇā nano jinavaro, samaṇīva siluccayo;

Karuṇāpuṇṇahadayo, vivaṭṭo viya sāgaro.

The Victorious One with a golden countenance, like the Himālaya, a mountain of rock; with a heart full of compassion, like the wide-open ocean.

4.

4.

Lokavissutakittīva, sineruva naguttamo;

Yasasā vitato dhīro, ākāsasadiso muni.

With fame renowned throughout the world, like Sineru, best of mountains; the wise one, the sage, is vast in glory, like the sky.

5.

5.

Asaṅgacitto sabbattha, anilo viya nāyako;

Patiṭṭhā sabbabhūtānaṃ, mahīva munisuttamo.

With mind unattached everywhere, the leader is like the wind; a support for all beings, the supreme sage is like the earth.

6.

6.

Anūpalitto lokena, toyena padumaṃ yathā;

Kuvādagacchadahano, aggikkhandhova sobhati.

Unsullied by the world, as a lotus is by water; he is a burner of the thicket of wrong views, shining like a mass of fire.

7.

7.

Agado viya sabbattha, kilesavisanāsako;

Gandhamādanaselova, guṇagandhavibhūsito.

Like a universal antidote, he is the destroyer of the poison of defilements; like Mount Gandhamādana, he is adorned with the fragrance of virtues.

8.

8.

Guṇānaṃ ākaro dhīro, ratanānaṃva sāgaro;

Sindhūva vanarājīnaṃ, kilesamalahārako.

The wise one is a mine of virtues, as the ocean is of gems; he is the remover of the stain of defilements, as a great river sweeps through the forest.

9.

9.

Vijayīva mahāyodho, mārasenappamaddano;

Cakkavattīva so rājā, bojjhaṅgaratanissaro.

Like a victorious great warrior, he is the crusher of Māra’s army; like a wheel-turning monarch, he is the lord of the jewels of the enlightenment factors.

10.

10.

Mahābhisakkasaṅkāso, dosabyādhitikicchako;

Sallakatto yathā vejjā, diṭṭhigaṇḍaviphālako.

Resembling a great physician, he is the healer of the disease of hatred; like a surgeon, he is the one who lances the boil of wrong views.

11.

11.

Satthā [M 0.0096] no bhagavā deva, mahābrahmehi vandito;

Devindasurasiddhehi, vandanīyo sadā darā.

Our Teacher, the Blessed One, O King, is venerated by the great Brahmās; he is ever to be venerated with respect by the lords of devas, asuras, and siddhas.

12.

12.

Sabbesu cakkavāḷesu, ye aggā ye ca pūjitā;

Tesamaggo mahārāja, bhagavā no patāpavāti.

In all world-systems, among all who are chief and all who are honored, the foremost of them, O great king, is our Blessed One, the mighty one.

Ayuttaṃ deva devakulehi kataṃti āhaṃsu. Taṃ sutvā rājā bho sabbepi manussā attano attano devatānaṃ mahantabhāvaṃ kathenti. Tumhākaṃ pana satthuno mahantabhāvaṃ kathaṃ amhākaṃ jānāpethāti, upāsakā na garu tvaṃ mahārāja phalakaṃ āharāpetvā suddhavatthena veṭhetvā taṃ attano muddikāya lañchitvā surakkhitasugopite ekasmiṃ devakule ṭhapetvā sattāhaccayena āharāpetvā taṃ oloketha, tadā no satthuno mahantānubhāvaṃ jānāthāti āhaṃsu, atha rājā tesaṃ vuttaniyāmeneva kārāpetvā antodevakule ṭhapetvā sabbadvārāni pidahitvā lañchetvā rakkheyyāthāti niyojesi. Tato te upāsakā sabbe sannipatitvā sattāhaṃ dānaṃ dentā sīlaṃ rakkhantā uposathakammaṃ karontā sabbasattesu mettiṃ bhāventā sabbasattānaṃ attanā katapuññesu pattiṃ dentā tiṇṇaṃ ratanānaṃ pūjaṃ karontā evaṃ ugghosesuṃ. Amhākaṃ katakusalanissandena loke mahiddhikā mahānubhāvā sabbe devā ca lokaṃ pālentā cattāro mahārājāno ca amhākaṃ satthuno upaṭṭhānāya upaṭṭhāya itisabbattha ṭhitabhāvaṃ dassentūti saccakiriyaṃ akaṃsu. Atha tesaṃ puññānubhāvena tasmiṃ khaṇe sakkassa devarañño paṇḍukambalasilāsanaṃ uṇhākāraṃ dassesi. Tato so manussalokaṃ olokento micchādiṭṭhīhi kataṃ taṃ vippakāraṃ disvā saṃviggo āgantvā issaraṃ bhagavato pāde vanditvā sayitākāraṃ [M 0.0097] katvā taṃ pavattiṃ upāsakānaṃ kathetvā sakaṭṭhānameva agamāsi. Tato sattame divasepātova te sabbepi rañño santikaṃ gantvā vanditvā evamāhaṃsu. Deva issaro amhākaṃ bhagavato pāde sirasā vanditvā nipannoti. Atha rājā tesaṃ kathaṃ sutvā nagare bheriṃ carāpetvā mahājane sannipātetvā tehi parivuto devakulaṃ gantvā lañchaṃ bhindāpetvā dvāraṃ vivaritvā phalakaṃ āharāpetvā veṭhitasāṭake mocāpesi. Atha rājā ca mahājano ca taṃ mahantaṃ pāṭihāriyaṃ disvā micchādiṭṭhiṃ pahāya sabbe satthuno saraṇa magamaṃsu. Atha rājā taṃ devakulaṃ bhindāpetvā mahantaṃ ramaṇīyaṃ vihāraṃ kārāpetvā yāvajīvaṃ puññakammaṃ katvā devaloke nibbatti.

They said: 'Your Majesty, what has been done by the temple-keepers is improper.' Hearing this, the king said: 'Sirs, all people declare the greatness of their own deities. But how can you make us know the greatness of your Teacher?' The lay followers replied: 'It is not difficult, great king. Have the panel brought, wrap it in a clean cloth, seal it with your own signet ring, and place it in a well-secured and well-guarded shrine. After seven days have passed, have it brought out and look at it. Then you will know the great power of our Teacher.' The king had this done exactly as they had said. He had it placed inside the shrine, had all the doors closed and sealed, and appointed guards. Then all the lay followers assembled and for seven days gave alms, observed the precepts, undertook the Uposatha, developed loving-kindness towards all beings, shared the merit they had made with all beings, and paid homage to the Three Jewels. They made this act of truth: 'By the fruit of the wholesome deeds we have done, may all the devas of great power and might in the world, and the Four Great Kings who protect the world, show that they stand in service to our Teacher!' Then, by the power of their merit, at that moment the Paṇḍukambala stone seat of Sakka, king of the devas, became hot. Looking down upon the human world, he saw the offense committed by those with wrong views and was deeply stirred. He descended and made the image of Issara lie down in a posture of venerating the feet of the Blessed One. He then related this event to the lay followers and returned to his own abode. Then, on the morning of the seventh day, they all went to the king, paid homage, and said: 'Your Majesty, Issara is now lying prostrate, venerating the feet of our Blessed One with his head.' Hearing their words, the king had a drum beaten through the city to assemble the populace. Surrounded by a great crowd, he went to the shrine, had the seal broken, the door opened, the panel brought out, and the wrapping cloths removed. The king and the great crowd saw this great miracle, abandoned their wrong views, and all went for refuge to the Teacher. The king then had that shrine demolished and a great and beautiful monastery built. For the rest of his life he performed meritorious deeds and was reborn in the world of the devas.

13.

13.

Anabbhutaṃ satthu dharīyamāne,

Karonti disvā kusalāni iddhiṃ;

Ye taṃ muninde parinibbutamhi,

Karonti puññāni mahabbhūtaṃ yeti.

It is no marvel that the wise perform meritorious deeds when they see a miracle while the Teacher is still living. But that they perform meritorious deeds when the Lord of Sages has attained final Nibbāna—this is the great marvel.

Kāvīrapaṭṭanavatthuṃ pañcamaṃ.

The fifth story: that of Kāvīrapaṭṭana.

26. Coraghātakavatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

26. Herein is the sequential story concerning the executioner of thieves.

Ekasmiṃ kira samaye amhākaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ upanissāya jetavane viharati dhammadesanāya mahājanassa saggamokkhasampadaṃ dadamāno. Tasmiṃsamaye pañcasatā corā aṭavito nagaraṃ āgantvā rattibhāge corakammaṃ katvā tena puttadāre posenti. Athekadivasaṃ corā corakammatthāya nagaraṃ pavisantā nagaradvāre ekaṃ dukkhitaṃ janapadamanussaṃ passitvā hambho [M 0.0098] kattha vasatīti pucchiṃsu, so attajanā janapadavāsibhāvaṃ pakāsesi. Athassa te kasmā bho iminā dukkhavāsena vasissasi, ehi amhehi saddhiṃ corakammaṃ karonto vatthālaṅkārasampanno puttadāraṃ posehi. Iminā kapaṇavāsena na vasāti āhaṃsu. So panime yuttaṃ kathentīti tesaṃ vacanaṃ sampaṭicchi. Atha te evaṃ sati amhehi saddhiṃ āgacchāhīti vatvā naṃ gahetvā antonagaraṃ paviṭṭhā tattha tattha vilumpantā corakammaṃ akaṃsu. Tadā jānapadiko laddhavibhavo imameva varataranti tehi saddhiṃ corakammaṃ karonto jīvikaṃ kappesi, athekadivasaṃ rājapurisā katakamme te sabbeva gahetvā pacchābāhaṃ gāḷhaṃ bandhitvā kosalarañño dassesuṃ, rājā te disvā evamāha, bhaṇe tumhākaṃ antare yo etesaṃ māretvā jīvitakkhayaṃ pāpessati, tassa jīvitadānaṃ dammīti, taṃ sutvā te corā sabbe aññamaññañātisuhadasambandhabhāvena taṃ na icchiṃsu. So pana janapadavāsī manusso ahamete sabbe māressāmīti rañño vatvā tenānuññāto te sabbe māresi. Taṃ disvā tuṭṭho rājā tassa coraghātakammaṃ adāsi. So core ca vajjhappatte ca mārento pañcavīsativassāni vasanto aparabhāge mahallako ahosi. Atha so mandabalattā katipayapahārenāpi coraṃ māretuṃ na sakkoti, rājā taṃ ñatvā aññassa coraghātakammaṃ adāsi. Atha so coraghātakammā coraghātakamma itipikatthaci parihīno attano gehe vasati. Tadā aññataro manusso mantaṃ parivattetvā nāsāvātena manussamāraṇakamantaṃ jānāti. Tathāhi hatthapādakaṇṇanāsasīsādīsu yaṃkiñci chejjabhejjaṃ kattukāmo mantaṃ parivattetvā nāsāvātaṃ vissajjeti. Taṃ taṃ ṭhānaṃ chijjati bhijjati, evaṃ mahānubhāvo so manto, atha so taṃ purisaṃ upaṭṭhahitvā mantaṃ labhitvā rañño sāsanaṃ pesesi[M 0.0099]. Ahaṃ ito pubbe mahallakattā corānaṃ hatthapādādayo dukkhena chejja bhejjaṃ karomi, māretabbepi dukkhena māremi. Idāni panāhaṃ tathā na karomi, mama mantānubhāvena chejjabhejjakammaṃ karissāmīti. Rājā taṃ sāsanaṃ sutvā sādhūti taṃ pakkosāpetvā ṭhānantaraṃ tasseva pākatika makāsi. So tato paṭṭhāya taṃ kammaṃ karonto puna pañcavassāni atikkāmesi. So mahallako khīṇāyuko dubbalo maraṇamañcaparāyaṇo hutvā maraṇavedanādukkhena mahantena bhayānakena saddena vissaraṃ viravanto nimīlitena cakkhunā bhayānakaṃ narakaggijālāpajjalantaayakūṭamuggaradhare nirayapāle ca passanto nipanno hoti, tato tassa paṭivissakagehe manussā tassa bhayānakasaddasavaṇena gehaṃ chaṭṭetvā palāyiṃsu. Tasmiṃ kira divase mahāsāriputtatthero dibbacakkhunā lokaṃ olokentā taṃ coraghātakaṃ tadaheva kālaṃkatvā niraye nibbattamānaṃ disvā mayi tattha gate panesa mayi pasādena sagge nibbattatīti ñatvā ajja mayā tassānuggahaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭatīti pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā tassa gharadvāra magamāsi. Atha so theraṃ disvā kuddho kopena taṭataṭāyamānadeho ajja taṃ vijjhitvā phāletvā māressāmīti nipannova mantaṃ parivattetvā nāsāvātaṃ vissajjesi, thero tasmiṃ khaṇe nirodhasamāpanno nirodhā vuṭṭhāya suriyo viya virocamāno aṭṭhāsi, atha so therassa tayo vāre tatheva katvā kiñci kātuṃ asakkonto ativiya vimhitacitto there cittaṃ pasādetvā attano paṭiyattaṃ pāyasaṃ therassa dāpesi, thero maṅgalaṃ vaḍḍhetvā vihārameva agamāsi, coraghātako therassa dinnadānaṃ anussaranto tasmiṃ khaṇe kālaṃ katvā sagge nibbatti. Aho vītarāgānaṃ buddhaputtānaṃ ānubhāvo. Evaṃ narake nibbattamānopissa balena sagge nibbattoti. Tathāhi.

At one time, our Blessed One was dwelling near Sāvatthī in Jeta's Grove, bestowing upon a great multitude the attainment of heaven and liberation through his teaching of the Dhamma. At that time, five hundred thieves would come from the forest into the city. At night, they would commit robberies and support their wives and children with the proceeds. One day, as the thieves were entering the city to commit a robbery, they saw a distressed countryman at the city gate and asked, 'Friend, where do you live?' He revealed that he was a country-dweller. Then they said to him, 'Why, friend, do you live in such misery? Come, commit robberies with us, and adorned with clothes and ornaments, you can support your wife and children. Do not live in this wretched way!' Thinking, 'What they say is reasonable,' he accepted their proposal. Then they said, 'If that is so, come with us,' and taking him, they entered the city. There they plundered here and there, committing robberies. The countryman, having gained wealth, thought, 'This is far better,' and made his living by committing robberies with them. One day, the king’s men caught them all in the act, bound their hands tightly behind their backs, and presented them to the king of Kosala. Seeing them, the king said, 'Listen, whoever among you will kill these men and put an end to their lives, I will grant him his life.' Hearing this, all the thieves, because of their bonds of kinship and friendship, were unwilling to do it. But the country-dwelling man said to the king, 'I will kill them all,' and with the king’s permission, he killed them all. Seeing this, the pleased king gave him the post of executioner of thieves. For twenty-five years, he executed thieves and condemned criminals. In his later years, he became old. Then, because of his weakness, he was not able to kill a thief even with a few blows. The king, learning of this, gave the post of executioner to another. Deprived of his post, he lived in his own home. At that time, a certain man knew a spell for killing people with the breath from his nose, which he had mastered by recitation. Indeed, desiring to cut or break anything—hands, feet, ears, nose, head, and so on—he would recite the spell and release the breath from his nose, and that very place would be cut or broken. So powerful was that spell. Then the former executioner, having approached that man and obtained the spell, sent a message to the king: 'Previously, because of old age, I performed the cutting and breaking of the limbs of thieves with difficulty, and I killed those to be executed with difficulty. But now I will not do so; by the power of my spell, I will do the work of cutting and breaking.' The king, hearing that message, said, 'Good!' and having summoned him, he restored him to his former position. From then on, doing that work, he passed another five years. Now, as an old man, with his life-span exhausted, weak, and lying on his deathbed, he was overcome by the pain of death. Crying out discordantly with a great, terrifying sound, with his eyes closed, he lay seeing the frightful hell-guardians holding iron clubs and hammers, blazing with the flames of hellfire. Then the people in the neighboring houses, hearing his terrifying cries, abandoned their homes and fled. On that very day, it is said, the Elder Mahā Sāriputta, surveying the world with his divine eye, saw that executioner dying on that very day and being reborn in hell. But knowing, 'If I go there, this one, through confidence in me, will be reborn in heaven,' he thought, 'Today it is proper for me to help him.' In the forenoon, having dressed, he went to the door of the man's house. Seeing the elder, the man, furious, his body crackling with rage, thought, 'Today I will pierce him, split him, and kill him!' and while still lying down, he recited the spell and released the breath from his nose. At that moment, the elder entered the attainment of cessation. Having arisen from cessation, he stood shining like the sun. Then the man, having tried this against the elder three times and being unable to do anything, was greatly astonished. His mind filled with confidence in the elder, he had the milk-rice that had been prepared for him given to the elder. The elder, having given a blessing, returned to the monastery. The executioner, recollecting the gift he had given to the elder, died at that moment and was reborn in a heavenly world. Ah, the power of the sons of the Buddha who are free from lust! Thus, even one who was being reborn in hell was, by this power, reborn in heaven. Indeed.

1.

1.

Dānaṃ [M 0.0100] tāṇaṃ manussānaṃ, dānaṃ duggativāraṇaṃ;

Dānaṃ saggassa sopānaṃ, dānaṃ santikaraṃ paraṃ.

Giving is a refuge for humans, giving prevents bad destinations; giving is the stairway to heaven, giving is a maker of supreme peace.

2.

2.

Icchiticchitadānena, dānaṃ cintāmaṇī viya;

Kapparukkhova sattānaṃ, dānaṃ bhaddaghaṭoviya.

By giving what is wished for, giving is like a wish-fulfilling gem; like a wish-fulfilling tree for beings, giving is like a pot of good fortune.

3.

3.

Sīlavantassa dānena, cakkavattisirimpi ca;

Labhanti sakkasampattiṃ, tathā lokuttaraṃ sukhaṃ.

By giving to a virtuous one, they obtain the glory of a wheel-turning monarch, the fortune of Sakka, and also supramundane happiness.

4.

4.

Pāpakammesu nirato, ṭhitoyaṃ narakāyane narakāvane itipikatthaci;

Sāriputtassa therassa, piṇḍapātassa vāhasā.

This one, who delighted in evil deeds and stood on the path to hell, by the power of the alms-food given to the Elder Sāriputta,

5.

5.

Apāyaṃ parivajjetvā, nekadukkhasamākulaṃ;

Devasaṅghaparibbūḷho, gato devapuraṃ varaṃ.

...avoided the state of woe crowded with manifold suffering and, surrounded by a host of devas, went to the excellent city of the gods.

6.

6.

Tasmā sukhette saddhāya, detha dānāni kāmadaṃ;

Dānaṃ dentehi sīlampi, pālanīyaṃtisundaranti pālanaṃcātisundaraṃ itipikatthaci.

Therefore, with faith, give gifts that fulfill desires in an excellent field of merit; and by those who give gifts, virtue too should be observed—this is truly excellent.

Atha bhikkhū dhammasabhāyaṃ sannipatitvā nisinnā bhagavantaṃ pucchiṃsu, kiṃbhante so pāpo catūsu apāyesu katarasmiṃ nibbattoti. Atha satthā ajjesa bhikkhave sāriputtassa dinnadānānubhāvena devaloke nibbatto, tasseva nissandena anāgate paccekabuddho bhavissatīti byākāsīti.

Then the bhikkhus, having assembled and sat down in the hall of the Dhamma, asked the Blessed One: 'Venerable sir, in which of the four states of woe has that sinner been reborn?' Then the Teacher declared: 'Bhikkhus, today, by the power of the gift given to Sāriputta, he has been reborn in the deva world. And as a result of that same deed, in the future he will become a Paccekabuddha.'

7.

7.

Bho sāriputte nihitappadānaṃ,

Khaṇena pāpeti hi saggamaggaṃ;

Tasmā sukhettesu dadātha dānaṃ,

Kāmattha ce saggamokkhaṃ parattha.

Sirs, a gift placed in Sāriputta indeed leads to the path of heaven in an instant. Therefore, give gifts in excellent fields of merit, if you desire heaven and liberation in the hereafter.

Coraghātakavatthuṃ chaṭṭhamaṃ.

The Sixth Story: The Executioner of Thieves.

27. Saddhopāsakassa vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

27. This is the introductory discourse to the story of the faithful lay follower.

Atīte [M 0.0101] kira kassapadasabalassa kāle eko puriso saddho ratanattayesu pasanno ucchuyantakammena jīvikaṃ kappento paṭivasati. Atha so ekaṃ gilānabhikkhuṃ disvā tassa uḷuṅkamattaṃ sappiṃ adāsi, tathevekassa bhikkhussa ekaṃ guḷapiṇḍaṃ adāsi, athāparasmiṃdivase ekaṃ chātajjhattaṃ sunakhaṃ disvā tassa bhattapiṇḍena saṅgaha makāsi, athekassa iṇaṭṭhakassa ekaṃ kahāpaṇaṃ adāsi, athekadivasaṃ dhammaṃ suṇamāno dhammadesakassa bhikkhussa sāṭakaṃ pūjesi, so ettakaṃ puññakammaṃ katvā bhavesu caramānohaṃ samuddapabbatādīsupi yaṃ yaṃ icchāmi. Taṃ taṃ samijjhatūti patthanaṃ akāsi, so aparabhāge kālaṃ katvā teheva kusalamūlehi suttappabuddho viya devaloke nibbattitvā tattha mahantaṃ dibbasampattiṃ anubhavitvā tato cuto amhākaṃ bhagavato kāle sāvatthiyaṃ mahaddhane mahāsālakule nibbattitvā tato so viññutaṃ patto kālena kālaṃ dhammaṃ suṇanto gharāvāse ādīnavaṃ pabbajjāya ca ānisaṃsaṃ sutvā pabbajito na cireneva arahattaṃ pāpuṇi. So aparabhāge satthāraṃ vanditvā pañcasatabhikkhuparivāro ugganagaraṃ agamāsi, tattha seṭṭhino bhariyā saddhā ahosi pasannā. Sā theraṃ pañcahi bhikkhusatehi saddhiṃ bhikkhāya carantaṃ disvā turitaturitā gantvā therassa pattaṃ gahetvā saddhiṃ pañcasatehi bhikkhūhi bhojetvā theraṃ tattha nibaddhavāsatthaṃ yācitvā pañcasatakūṭāgārāni kārāpetvā alaṅkaritvā pañcasatabhikkhū tattha vāsentī nibaddhaṃ catupaccayehi upaṭṭhānamakāsi. Tato thero taṃ pañcasu sīlesu patiṭṭhāpetvā tattha yathābhirantaṃ viharitvā aññatta gantukāmo anupubbena paṭṭanagāmaṃ agamāsi, tattha vasitvā tato nāvaṃ abhiruyha pañcasatabhikkhūhi parivuto samuddapiṭṭhena gacchati, samuddaṃ tarantassa [M 0.0102] tassa sāgaramajjhe udaravāto samuṭṭhahitvā pīḷeti, taṃ disvā bhikkhū bhante idaṃ pubbe kena vūpasamessatīti pucchiṃsu, thero pubbe me āvuso uluṅkamatte sappipīte rogo vūpasammatīti āha, bhikkhū bhante samuddapiṭṭhe kathaṃ sappiṃ labhissāma, adhivāsethāti āhaṃsu, taṃ sutvā therena theronanoāyasmantā itisabbattha na no āyasmantā sappi dullabhā, mama pattaṃ gahetvā samuddodakaṃ uddharitvā ānethāti vutte bhikkhū tathā akaṃsu. Uddhaṭamattameva taṃudakaṃ parivattetvā sappi ahosi, atha bhikkhū taṃ disvā acchariyabbhutacittā jātā therassa sappiṃ upanāmesuṃ, therena sappino sappinā itisabbattha pītamatte so ābādho vūpasami, athassa bhikkhūhi ki metaṃ bhante acchariyaṃ, na no ito pubbe evarūpaṃ diṭṭhapubbaṃti vutte thero tenahi katapuññānaṃ puññavipākaṃ passissathāti vatvā samuddaṃ olokesi idaṃ sappi hotūti. Athassa cakkhupathe samudde sabbodakaṃ parivattetvā sappi ahosi. Athassa bhikkhū abbhutacittā aññampi īdisaṃ puññaṃ atthi bhanteti pucchiṃsu, tato thero tenahi passathāyasmantā mama puññanti vatvā samantā tattha tattha ghanaselapabbate olokesi, sabbāni tāni guḷapiṇḍāni ahesuṃ, tato cakkhupathe samantā bhattabhājanāni dassesi sabyañjanaṃ sopakaraṇaṃ. Tato himavantaṃ olokesi, sabbaṃ taṃ suvaṇṇamayaṃ ahosi. Athābhimukhaṭṭhāne mahantaṃ vanasaṇḍaṃ olokesi, sakalavanasaṇḍaṃnānāvirāgavatthehi sañchannaṃ ahosi, bhikkhū taṃ taṃ pāṭihāriyaṃ disvā atīva vimhitā bhante kena te puññakammena etādisāni pāṭihāriyāni bhavissantīti pucchiṃsu, thero kassapadasabalassa kāle attanā kataṃ sabbaṃ taṃ kusalaṃ pakāsesi. Tenettha.

In the past, it is said, during the time of Kassapa of the Ten Powers, there lived a faithful man with confidence in the Triple Gem, who made his living by operating a sugarcane press. One day, seeing a sick monk, he gave him a ladleful of clarified butter. Similarly, to another monk, he gave a lump of molasses. The next day, seeing a starving dog, he assisted it with a ball of rice. To a debtor, he gave a single kahāpaṇa. Another day, while listening to the Dhamma, he honored the monk who was teaching with a gift of cloth. Having performed these meritorious deeds, he made an aspiration: 'As I wander through existences, may whatever I desire be fulfilled, even amidst oceans and mountains.' After his death, on account of these wholesome roots, he was reborn in a deva world as if awakening from sleep, and there he enjoyed great divine bliss. After passing away from there, he was reborn in a very wealthy, prominent family in Sāvatthī during the time of our Blessed One. Having come of age, he regularly listened to the Dhamma, and having heard of the danger in the household life and the benefit of renunciation, he eventually went forth. Not long after, he attained Arahantship. Later, after paying homage to the Teacher, he set out for the city of Ugga with a retinue of five hundred monks. There, the wife of a treasurer was faithful and confident. Seeing the elder and his five hundred monks on their alms round, she hurried over, took the elder's bowl, and served a meal to him along with the five hundred monks. She then requested the elder to reside there permanently, had five hundred gabled chambers built and adorned for them, and continuously attended to the five hundred monks with the four requisites. The elder established her in the five precepts and dwelled there for as long as he wished. When he later wished to travel elsewhere, he gradually reached the port town of Paṭṭanagāma. After staying there, he boarded a ship with his five hundred monks and sailed across the ocean. While crossing the sea, a wind in his stomach arose and afflicted him. Seeing this, the monks asked, 'Venerable sir, how was this pacified previously?' The elder replied, 'Friends, in the past, the illness was pacified by drinking a ladleful of clarified butter.' The monks said, 'Venerable sir, how can we obtain clarified butter in the middle of the ocean? Please endure it.' Hearing this, the elder said, 'Venerable sirs, clarified butter is not hard for me to obtain. Take my bowl, draw up some seawater, and bring it here.' The monks did so. As soon as it was drawn up, the water transformed into clarified butter. Seeing this, the monks, their minds filled with wonder and amazement, offered the clarified butter to the elder. As soon as the elder drank the clarified butter, his ailment subsided. The monks asked, 'Venerable sir, what is this marvel? We have never seen such a thing before.' The elder replied, 'Then you shall see the fruit of merit for one who has made merit.' He then gazed at the ocean and resolved, 'Let this be clarified butter.' Instantly, all the water within his range of vision turned into clarified butter. The monks, astonished, asked, 'Venerable sir, do you have other such merits?' The elder replied, 'Then see my merit, venerable sirs,' and looked around at the dense rocky mountains. All of them turned into lumps of molasses. Then, within his range of vision, he displayed food vessels complete with curries and condiments. Next, he gazed at the Himālaya, and the entire range turned to gold. Looking at a great forest grove ahead, the entire grove became covered with cloths of various colors. Seeing these various miracles, the monks were utterly astounded and asked, 'Venerable sir, by what meritorious deed have such marvels come to be?' The elder then revealed all the wholesome deeds he had performed during the time of Kassapa of the Ten Powers. Therefore:

1.

1.

Imasmiṃ [M 0.0103] bhaddake kappe, kassapo nāma nāyako;

Sabbalokahitatthāya, loke uppajji cakkhumā.

In this auspicious aeon, a leader named Kassapa, the One with Vision, arose in the world for the welfare of all the world.

2.

2.

Tadāhaṃ ucchuyantamhi, niyutto guḷakārako;

Tena kammena jīvāmi, posento puttadārake.

At that time, I was a molasses-maker employed at a sugarcane press; by that work I made my living, supporting my wife and children.

3.

3.

Kilantindriyamaddakkhiṃ, bhikkhuṃ rogāturaṃ tadā;

Bhikkhācārakavattena, ghatatthaṃ ghatamattamupāgataṃ itisabbattha samupāgataṃ.

Then I saw a monk with weary faculties, afflicted by illness, who had come for ghee in the course of his alms round.

4.

4.

Uluṅkamattaṃ sappissa, adadaṃ tassa bhikkhuno;

Saddahanto dānaphalaṃ, dayāyu daggamānaso.

A ladleful of ghee I gave to that monk, trusting in the fruit of giving, compassionate, with a generous mind.

.5

.5

Tena kammena saṃsāre, saṃsaranto bhavābhave;

Yatthicchāmi ghataṃ tattha, uppajjati anappakaṃ.

By that deed, while wandering in saṃsāra from existence to existence, wherever I wish for ghee, it arises in abundance.

6.

6.

Icchāmahaṃ samuddasmiṃ, phalampi ghatamattano;

Taṃ taṃ sabbaṃ ghataṃ hoti, ghatadānassidaṃ phalaṃ.

Should I wish for it in the ocean, it all becomes ghee for me; this is the fruit of my gift of ghee.

7.

7.

Suṇātha mayhaṃ aññampi, puññakammaṃ manoramaṃ;

Tadā disvānahaṃ bhikkhuṃ, rogena paripīḷitaṃ.

Listen to another delightful meritorious deed of mine; then I saw a monk greatly afflicted by illness.

8.

8.

Guḷapiṇḍaṃ gahetvāna, patte tassa samākiriṃ;

Tena so sukhito āsi, rogaṃ byapagataṃ tadā.

Taking a lump of molasses, I placed it in his bowl. Through that he became well, and his illness then disappeared.

9.

9.

Tena me guḷadānena, saṃsaraṃ devamānuse;

Yatthatthosmi guḷenāhaṃ, tattha taṃ sulabhaṃ mama.

By this gift of molasses of mine, as I wander among devas and humans, wherever I have need of molasses, it is easily obtained by me.

10.

10.

Selāca vipulā mayhaṃ, honti cittānuvattakā;

Mahantaguḷapiṇḍāva, guḷadāne idaṃ phalaṃ.

Vast mountains become obedient to my will, turning into great lumps of molasses. This is the fruit of the gift of molasses.

11.

11.

Athāpi me kataṃ puññaṃ, suṇātha sādhu bhikkhavo;

Chātajjhattaṃ phandamānaṃ, disvāna sunakhaṃ tadā.

And further, monks, listen well to a merit I performed: I then saw a dog trembling, overcome by hunger.

12.

12.

Bhattapīṇḍena [M 0.0104] saṅgaṇhiṃ, tampi dānaṃ phalāvahaṃ;

Tato paṭṭhāya nāhosi, annapānena ūnatā.

I assisted it with a lump of rice; that gift too bore its fruit. From that time on, there was no lack of food and drink for me.

13.

13.

Sulabhannapāno sukhito, ahosiṃjātijātiyaṃ;

Ajjāpi yadi icchāmi, bhojanena payojanaṃ.

Happy, with easily obtained food and drink, I was in birth after birth. Even today, if I have need of food,

14.

14.

Cakkhupathe samantā me, jāyantukkhaliyo bahū;

Athāparampi kusalaṃ, akāsiṃtaṃ saṇātha me.

many cooking pots arise all around me within my range of vision. And I did another skillful deed; listen to it from me.

15.

15.

Iṇaṭṭhakassa posassa, adāsekaṃ kahāpaṇaṃ;

Tena me puññakammena, anomabhavasampadaṃ.

To a man afflicted by debt I gave a single kahāpaṇa; through that meritorious deed of mine, matchless prosperity in existence...

16.

16.

Pacuraṃ jātarūpañca, labhāmi jātijātiyaṃ;

Sacajja dhanakāmohaṃ, ghanaselopi pabbato;

Hoti hemamayaṃ sabbaṃ, iṇato mocane phalaṃ.

...and abundant gold I obtain in birth after birth. If today I desire wealth, even a solid rocky mountain becomes entirely golden. This is the fruit of releasing someone from debt.

17.

17.

Aññampi mama puññaṃ bho, suṇātha sutisobhanaṃ;

Kassapassa bhagavato, sāsanekaṃ bahussutaṃ.

Sirs, listen to another merit of mine, which is pleasant to hear, from the Dispensation of the Blessed One Kassapa.

18.

18.

Desentaṃ munino dhammaṃ, sutvā pīṇitamānaso;

Pūjesiṃsāṭakaṃ mayhaṃ, dhammassa dhammasāmino.

Having heard a learned one teaching the Dhamma of the Sage, with a gladdened mind, I honored the Lord of the Dhamma with my own cloth.

19.

19.

Tenāhaṃ puññakammena, saṃsaraṃ devamānuse;

Labhāmi pacuraṃ vatthaṃ, yaṃ lokasmiṃ varaṃ paraṃ.

By that meritorious deed, as I wander among devas and humans, I obtain abundant cloth, whatever is excellent and supreme in the world.

20.

20.

Icchamāno sace ajja jānamānopahaṃajja; jānadhānocahaṃajja itikatthaci, himavantampi pabbataṃ;

Nānāvirāgavatthehi, chādayissaṃ samantato.

If I so wished today, I could cover even the Himālaya mountain all around with cloths of various colors.

21.

21.

Sace icchāmi ajjeva, vatthenacchādayā mite;

Jantavo catudīpasmiṃ, vatthadānassidaṃ phalaṃ.

If I so wished this very day, I could cover with cloth all the beings in the four continents. Such is the fruit of the gift of cloth.

22.

22.

Etesaṃ puññakammānaṃ, vāhasā kāmabhūmiyaṃ;

Sampatti manubhutvāna, sāvatthipura muttame.

By the power of these meritorious deeds, having experienced prosperity in the sense-sphere realm, in the supreme city of Sāvatthī...

23.

23.

Jāto kule mahābhoge,

Vuddhippatto sukhedhito;

Tassa dhammaṃ suṇitvāna,

Pabbajitvāna sāsane.

...I was born in a family of great wealth. Having grown up nurtured in happiness, and having heard his Dhamma, I went forth into the Dispensation.

24.

24.

Lokuttaraṃ aggarasaṃ, bhuñcanto munivāhasā;

Kilese pajahitvāna, arahattamapāpuṇiṃ.

Enjoying the supreme, supramundane taste by the Sage's power, having abandoned the defilements, I attained Arahantship.

25.

25.

Kusalaṃ nā vamantabbaṃ, khuddakanti kadācipi;

Anantaphaladaṃ hoti, nibbāṇampi dadāti taṃ.

A wholesome deed should never be despised, thinking, 'It is small.' It is productive of endless fruit; it even gives Nibbāna.

Athassa dhammadesanaṃ sutvā bhikkhū ca mahājano ca dānādikusalakammaṃ katvā yebhuyyena saggaparāyaṇā ahesuṃti.

Then, having heard his Dhamma discourse, the monks and the great assembly of people performed wholesome deeds such as giving and, for the most part, had heaven as their destination.

26.

26.

Manopasādenapi appapuññaṃ,

Evaṃ mahantaṃ bhavatīti ñatvā;

Mā appapuññanti pamajjathambho;

Sarātha deviṃ idha lājadāyiṃ.

Knowing that even a little merit done with a confident mind becomes so great, do not be negligent, sirs, thinking, 'It is a small merit.' Remember the devī here who gave the roasted grain.

Saddhopāsakassa vatthuṃ sattamaṃ.

The seventh story of the faithful lay follower.

28. Kapaṇassa vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

28. This is the sequential discourse on the story of the poor man.

Amhākaṃ bhagavati parinibbute bārāṇasīnagaravāsī eko duggatapuriso paragehe bhatiṃkatvā jīvikaṃ kappeti, tasmiṃ samaye nagaravāsino yebhuyyena tasmiṃ tasmiṃ ṭhāne maṇḍapādayo kārāpetvā mahādānaṃ denti, taṃ disvā duggato evaṃ cintesi, ahaṃ pubbe akatapuññattā paragehe bhatiṃ katvā kicchena kasirena jīvāmi. Nivāsanapārupanampi vāsaṭṭhānamattammi dukkhato labhāmi. Idāni buddhuppādo vattati bhikkhusaṅghopi.

After our Blessed One had attained final Nibbāna, a poor man living in the city of Bārāṇasī was making his living by working for others. At that time, the city-dwellers for the most part had pavilions and the like built in various places and were giving great alms. Seeing this, the poor man thought: “Because I made no merit in the past, I eke out a living with pain and difficulty by working for others. I obtain even clothing and a mere place to live with hardship. Now, the dispensation of a Buddha is present, and the Saṅgha of bhikkhus too.”

Sabbe [M 0.0106] ime dānaṃ datvā saggamaggaṃ sodhenti. Mayāpi dānaṃ dātuṃ vaṭṭati. Tamme dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya bhavissati. Api ca mayhaṃ taṇḍulanālimattampi natthi, akatavīriyena taṃ matthakaṃ pāpetuṃ na sakkā, etadatthāyāhaṃ uyyogaṃ katvā dānaṃ dassāmīti cintetvā tato paṭṭhāya bhatiṃ pariyesamāno gantvā tattha tattha bhatiṃ katvā laddhanivāpe ca bhikkhācariyāya laddhatilataṇḍulādayo ca ekattha saṃharitvā manusse samādāpetvā tasmiṃ maṇḍapaṃ kārāpetvā vanakusamādīhi taṃ alaṅkaritvā bhikkhū nimantetvā maṇḍape nisīdāpetvā sabbesaṃ pāyasaṃ pāyāsaṃ itisabbattha paṭiyādetvā bhojesi. Atha so maraṇakāle attanā kataṃ taṃ dānavaraṃ anussari. So tena kusalakammena suttappabuddho viya devaloke nibbatti. Tasmiṃ tena katapuññānurūpaṃ mahantaṃ kanakavimānaṃ nibbatti. Samantā tigāvutaṭṭhāne devatā nānā vidhāni turiyāni gahetvā upahāraṃ karonti. Niccaṃ devaccharāsahassāni devaccharāsahassaṃ itisabbattha taṃ parivāretvā tiṭṭhanti. Evaṃ so mahantaṃ sampattiṃ anubhavati. Athekadivasaṃ suvaṇṇaselavihāravāsī mahāsaṅgharakkhitatthero pattapaṭisambhido devacārikaṃ caramāno taṃ devaputtaṃ anupamāya devasampattiyā virocamānaṃ disvā upasaṅkamma ṭhito tena katakammaṃ pucchi. Sopissa yathābhūtaṃ byākāsi, tenattha.

“All these people, having given alms, are clearing the path to heaven. It is proper for me too to give a gift. That will be for my welfare and happiness for a long time. Yet I do not have even a nāḷi-measure of rice. Without making an effort, it is not possible to bring this to accomplishment. For this purpose, I will make an effort and give a gift.” Thinking thus, from then on, seeking wage-work, he went here and there and worked for hire. He collected in one place the provisions he received and what he obtained by begging for alms—sesame, rice, and so on. Having persuaded people to help, he had a pavilion built there, adorned it with wild flowers and the like, invited the bhikkhus, had them seated in the pavilion, prepared rice-porridge for all, and served them. Then, at the time of his death, he recollected that excellent gift he had given. By that wholesome kamma, as if awakening from sleep, he was reborn in the deva world. There a great golden mansion arose for him in accordance with the merit he had made. All around, in a space of three gāvutas, devatās make offerings, playing various kinds of musical instruments. Constantly, thousands of celestial nymphs surround and attend him. Thus he experiences great prosperity. Then one day, the Elder Mahāsaṅgharakkhita, a resident of the Golden Rock Monastery who had attained the analytical knowledges, was wandering among the devas. Seeing that devaputta shining with incomparable divine fortune, he approached, stood by, and asked about the kamma he had done. The devaputta explained it to him as it really was. Hence it was said:

1.

1.

Sabbasovaṇṇayo āsi, pāsādo ratanāmayo;

Soṇṇasiṅgasatākiṇṇo, duddikkho ca pabhassaro.

The palace was entirely of gold and made of jewels; filled with hundreds of golden pinnacles, it was difficult to behold and radiant.

2.

2.

Kūṭāgāra satākiṇṇo, soṇṇamālāsamākulo;

Muttākalāpālambanti, tattha tattha manoramā.

Filled with hundreds of gabled chambers and adorned with golden garlands, delightful festoons of pearls hang down here and there.

3.

3.

Nekagabbhasatākiṇṇo, sayanāsanamaṇḍito;

Vibhatto bhabbabhāgehi, puññavaḍḍhakinā kato.

Filled with many hundreds of chambers and adorned with couches and seats, it was divided into suitable portions, fashioned by the artificer of merit.

4.

4.

Naccanti [M 0.0107] pamadā tattha, bherimaṇḍalamajjhagā;

Gāyanti kāci kīḷanti, vādenti kāci tantiyo.

There damsels dance amidst a circle of drums; some sing, some play, and some strum their lutes.

5.

5.

Tato tigāvute ṭhāne, pāsādassa samantato;

Sahaccharā devaputtā, gahetvā ātatādayo.

Then, in a space of three gāvutas all around the palace, attendant devaputtas take up drums and other instruments.

6.

6.

Modanti parivāretvā, naccagītādinā sadā;

Ullaṅghantica selenti, silāghanti samantato.

Surrounding him, they always rejoice with dance, song, and music; they leap up and make merry, praising him from all around.

7.

7.

Evaṃ mahiddhiko dāni, tuvaṃ vandova bhāsati;

Pucchāmi taṃ devaputta, kiṃ kammamakarī purā.

“You are now so powerful, devaputta, shining like the moon. I ask you: what kamma did you perform in the past?”

Devaputto āha.

The devaputta said:

8.

8.

Ahosiṃ duggato pubbe, bārāṇasīpuruttame;

Dānaṃ denti narā tattha, nimantetvāna bhikkhavo.

“In the past I was a poor man in the excellent city of Bārāṇasī. There people would give alms, having invited the bhikkhus.

9.

9.

Jīvanto bhatiyā sohaṃ, dānaṃ dente mahājane;

Tuṭṭhahaṭṭhe pamudite, evaṃ cintesahaṃ tadā.

While I was living on wages, I saw the great folk giving alms, pleased, glad, and joyful, and then I thought to myself:

10.

10.

Sampannavatthālaṅkārā, dānaṃ denti ime janā;

Paratthapi pahaṭṭhāva, sampattimanubhonti te.

‘These people, endowed with fine clothes and ornaments, give alms; and joyful also in the next world, they will experience good fortune.

11.

11.

Buddhuppādo ayaṃ dāni, dhammo loke pavattati;

Susīlā dāni vattanti, dakkhiṇeyyā jinorasā.

Now is the age of a Buddha’s appearance; the Dhamma prevails in the world. Now the virtuous ones are living—the sons of the Victor, worthy of offerings.

12.

12.

Anāvaṭṭhito avaṭṭhitoca; andhaṭṭhitoca itipikattaci saṃsāro, apāyā khalu pūritā;

Kalyāṇavimukhā sattā, kāmaṃ gacchanti duggatiṃ.

Saṃsāra has not been brought to a halt; indeed, the planes of misery are full. Beings who turn away from the good go willingly to a woeful destination.

13.

13.

Idāni dukkhito hutvā, jīvāmi kasirenahaṃ;

Daliddo kapaṇo dīno, appabhogo anālayo.

Now I am afflicted and live with hardship; poor, wretched, and miserable, with little wealth and no home.

14.

14.

Idāni bījaṃ ropemi, sukhette sādhusammate;

Appevanāma tenāhaṃ, parattha sukhito siyā.

Now I shall plant a seed in a good field, one esteemed by the virtuous. Perhaps by this I might be happy in the hereafter.’

15.

15.

Iti cintiya bhikkhitvā, bhatiṃ katvāna nekadhā;

Maṇḍapaṃ tattha kāretvā, nimantetvāna bhikkhavo.

Having thought thus, I begged and worked for wages in many ways. Having had a pavilion built there, I invited the bhikkhus.

16.

16.

Āyāsena [M 0.0108] adāsāhaṃ, pāyasaṃ amatāya so;

Tena kammavipākena, devaloke manorame.

With toil I gave rice-porridge for the sake of the Deathless. By the ripening of that kamma, in the delightful deva world…

17.

17.

Jātomhi dibbakāmehi, modamāno anekadhā;

Dīghāyuko vaṇṇavanto, tejassīca ahosahanti.

…I have been born, rejoicing in many ways in divine sensual pleasures. I have become long-lived, beautiful, and powerful.”

Evaṃ devaputto attanā katapuññakammaṃ vittārena kathesi, theropi manussalokaṃ āgantvā manussānaṃ attanā paccakkhato diṭṭhadibbasampattiṃ pakāsesi. Taṃ sutvā mahājano kusalakammaṃ katvā yebhuyyena sagge nibbattoti.

Thus the devaputta related in detail the meritorious kamma he had performed. The elder, having returned to the human world, proclaimed to the people the divine fortune he had witnessed with his own eyes. Hearing this, the great populace performed wholesome kamma and were for the most part reborn in heaven.

18.

18.

Anālayo duggatadīnakopi,

Dānaṃ dadanto dhigato visesaṃ;

Saggā pavaggaṃ yadi patthayavho,

Hantvāna maccheramalaṃ dadāthāti.

Even one who is homeless, poor, and miserable, by giving a gift, attained distinction. If you aspire to heaven and release, then, having destroyed the stain of stinginess, you should give.

Kapaṇassa vatthuṃ aṭṭhamaṃ.

The Eighth Story: The Poor Man.

29. Devaputtassa vatthumhi ayamānupubbīkathā

29. This is the consecutive account in the story of the devaputta.

Ito pubbe nāradassa kira sammāsambuddhassa kāle ayaṃ dīpo aññatarena nāmena pākaṭo ahosi, so panekasmiṃ kāle dubbhikkho ahosi dussasso, mahāchātakabhayaṃ satte pīḷeti. Tasmiṃ samaye nāradassa bhagavato eko sāsaniko sāvako aññatarasmiṃ gāme piṇḍāya caritvā yathā dhotapattova nikkhami. Athaññatarasmiṃ gehe manussā ekaṃ taṇḍulanāḷiṃpoṭalikāya bandhitvā udake pakkhipitvā pacitvā udakaṃ gahetvā pivanto jīvanti, tadā theraṃ disvā vanditvā pattaṃ gahetvā tena taṇḍulena bhattaṃ pacitvā patte pakkhipitvā therassa adaṃsu. Atha [M 0.0109] tesaṃ saddhābalena sā ukkhali bhattena paripuṇṇā ahosi, te taṃ abbhutaṃ disvā ayyassa dinnadāne vipāko ajjeva no diṭṭhoti somanassajātā mahājanaṃ sannipātetvā te bhattaṃ bhojetvā pacchā sayaṃ bhuñjiṃsu. Bhattassa gahitagahitaṭṭhānaṃ pūrateva. Tato paṭṭhāya te sampattamahājanassa dānaṃ dadantā āyupariyosāne devaloke nibbattiṃsu, atha so thero bhattaṃ ādāya gantvā aññatarasmiṃ rukkhamūle nisīditvā bhuñjitumārati. Tasmiṃ kira rukkhe nibbatto eko devaputto āhārena kilanto bhuñjamānaṃ theraṃ disvā attabhāvaṃ vijahitvā mahallakavesena tassa samīpe aṭṭhāsi. Thero anāvajjitvāva bhuñjati. Devaputto carimālopaṃ ṭhapetvā bhuttakāle ukkāsitvā attānaṃ ṭhitabhāvaṃ jānāpesi. Thero taṃ disvā vippaṭisāri hutvā carimaṃ bhatthapiṇḍaṃ tassa hatthe ṭhapesi, tato so bhattapiṇḍaṃ gahetvā ṭhito cintesi. Ito kira mayā pubbe samaṇabrāhmaṇānaṃ vā kapaṇaddhikānaṃ vā antamaso kākasunakhā dīnampi āhāraṃ adinnapubbaṃ bhavissati, tenavāhaṃ devo hutvāpi bhattaṃ na labhāmi. Handāhaṃ imaṃ bhattapiṇḍaṃ therasseva dassāmi, taṃ me bhavissati dīgharattaṃ hitāya sukhāya cāti. Evañca pana cintetvā bhattapiṇḍe āsaṃ pahāya theraṃ upasaṅkamma sāmi dāsassa vo alaṃ idha lokena saṅgahaṃ. Paralokena me saṅgahaṃ karothāti vatvā tassa patte okiri. Athassa bhatthapiṇḍaṃ bhatthapiṇḍaṃ itisabbattha patte patitamatteyeva tigāvutaṭṭhāne dibbamayāni bhattabhājanānipaññāyiṃsu. Devaputto theraṃ pañcapatiṭṭhitena vanditvā tato dibbabhojanaṃ gahetvā pathamaṃ dānaṃ datvā pacchā sayaṃ bhuñci. Tato deputto dutiyadivasato paṭṭhāya therassa ca sampattamahājanassa ca mahādānaṃ dento āyupariyosāne [M 0.0110]0 devaloke nibbattitvā chasu kāmasaggesu aparāparaṃ dibbasampatti manubhavamāno padumuttarassa bhagavato kāle tato cuto bārāṇasiyaṃ anekavibhavassa micchādiṭṭhikassa kuṭumbikassa gehe nibbatti. Devotissa nāmaṃ akaṃsu. Aparabhāge viññutaṃ pattassa tassa mātāpitaro kālamakaṃsu. Sovaṇṇamaṇimuttādipūritakoṭṭhāgārādayo oloketvā mama mātāpitaro micchādiṭṭhikattā ito dānādikiñcikammaṃ akaritvā paralokaṃ gacchantā kākaṇikamattampi agahetvā gatā, ahaṃ pana taṃ gahetvāva gamissāmīti sanniṭṭhānaṃ katvā bheriṃ carāpetvā kapaṇaddhikavanibbake sannipātetvā sattāhabbhantare sabbaṃ sāpateyyaṃ dānamukhena datvā araññaṃ pavisitvā isippabbajjaṃ pabbajitvā kasiṇaparikammaṃ katvā pañca bhiññā aṭṭha samāpattiyo nibbattetvā ākāsacārī ahosi. Atha tasmiṃsamaye padumuttaro nāma bhagavā haṃsavatīnagare paṭivasanto devabrahmādiparivuto catusaccapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ dhammaṃ desento nisinno hoti, tadā so tāpaso ākāsena gacchanto mahājanasamāgamañca bhagavato sarīrato nikkhantachabbaṇṇaraṃsiyo ca disvā kimetaṃti vimhito ākāsā otaritvā mahatiyā buddhalīḷāya nisīditvā dhammaṃ desentaṃ bhagavantaṃ disvā pasannamānaso parisantare nisinno dhammaṃ sutvā bhagavantaṃ vanditvā attano assamameva agamāsi. Atha so tattha yāvatāyukaṃ ṭhatvā āyupariyosāne tāvatiṃsabhavane nibbatto tiṃsakappe dibbasampattimanubhavanto chasu kāmasaggesu aparāpariyavasena saṃsari. Ekapaññāsaattabhāve sakko devarājā ahosi, ekakavīsatiattabhāve cakkavatti hutvā manussasampatti manubhavitvā imasmiṃbuddhuppāde bhagavati parinibbute sāvatthiyaṃ aññatarasmiṃ kulagehe nibbattitvā sattavassiko ekaṃ bhikkhuṃ dhammaṃ desentaṃ addasa.
